# YKI level 3 exam-first curriculum, part 3: Days 57–84 and After Day 84

[Unreviewed Swedish: have a teacher check it]

This part covers Days 57–84: the last week of Phase 3 (topic transfer, Days 57–63, ending with the Phase 3 exit test on Days 62–63) and all of Phase 4 (timed full tasks and mocks, Days 64–84, ending with Mock 3 on Day 84). The final section, "After Day 84", gives the weekly repeat cycle and the 7-day taper (Phase 5).

IDs follow the main plan: templates T1–T16, grammar G1–G10, reasons 1–40, reactions 1–30, rescue moves 1–8, and appendix prompts A1–L10 (the Swedish cards are in the plan's appendix; this part gives the ID and a short English gloss, and writes out in full only the prompts it adds itself, marked [Plan's own]). "Engine errors" means G1, G2, G3 and G5. Appendix prompts already used in parts 1–2, or too close to their tasks, are not reused here.

A normal day is 60 minutes: review 0–8, new 8–18, speaking 18–33, writing 33–48, listening or reading 48–56, error log 56–60. Full-set and test days run longer, and their length is given honestly. Tags: [Official sample] = the public practice tasks; [Teacher, unofficial] = a class teacher's format; [Book count] = one practice book; [Plan's own] = a practice target set by this plan, not an exam rule. Blocks marked **seen practice** hold prompts that are printed in this file, so they are not unseen: a helper changes their details, and mocks that count towards booking use fresh prompts instead (plan 6.2). A helper plays the recorded voice in calls; with no helper, write 6 short questions for the other side first and cover them.

---

### Day 57: error clinic after Mock 2 (Phase 3, week 9)

Day 57: see section 5.1 of the plan.
It turns Mock 2's errors into work: the 12-sentence engine drill (wrong, then right) plus 2 new correct sentences for each of your own top 3 error types, then a repair-call redo (*Ditt kylskåp är fortfarande trasigt …*, T4, 7 points) and a 15-minute bill complaint to a painting firm (7 points, amounts that add up).
Any error type seen 3 or more times this week is marked red (3.6). This is the format every later fix day copies.

[Unreviewed Swedish: have a teacher check it]

- `p87-R4`: p. 87, Reagera 4 (phone), T4: Call payroll about a mistake in your pay
- `p96-D1`: p. 96-97, Dialog 1 (phone), T4: Call the library: overdue notice for a book you never borrowed; deny it.

### Day 58: weakest function A (Phase 3, week 9)

**What we learn today:**
- Choose today's function from Mock 2. Count, per function, the missing points and the teacher's ratings. The weakest is the one with the most missing points; on a tie, the lower rating; on a second tie, the one that is more frequent in the practice book (request 38 and complaint 33 of 133 tasks [Book count]).
- Say that function's template card aloud twice (T2, T3, T4/T5, T9, T10, T13, T14/T15 or T6/T7). If its points keep going missing, add the capital-letter line to the top of the card (3.6, rule 4).
- Then the helper gives you the matching row of the menu below.

**Menu for Days 58, 59 and 76 (seen practice).** One row per day. Rows not used stay available for Day 76 and After Day 84. If the weak function is a written one, use the row of its spoken twin: W3 complaint → R3, W2 letter → R6, W1 event message → R8.

| Row | Function | 3 speaking prompts | 1 writing prompt |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Request, booking (T2) | A1 book a hairdresser; A3 extend a library loan; A9 ask shop staff for help with a gift | A2 gym: price, opening hours, free trial |
| R2 | Buying and selling (T3) | B7 buyer, used computer; B9 seller, skis, bargain; B10 buyer, flat for rent | B8 buyer's message about a bookshelf |
| R3 | Complaint (T4, T5) | C2 pizza has not come; C4 tradesperson did not come; C9 dry cleaner lost your suit | D9 late delivery although you paid for express |
| R4 | Late, cancel, apology (T9) | F1 late for a job interview (voicemail); F3 cancel the hairdresser (voicemail); [Plan's own] voicemail: *Du har köpt ett bord och ska hämta det hos säljaren klockan fem, men bilen startar inte. Ring och lämna ett meddelande.* | F2 you can't come to the Swedish course; ask about homework |
| R5 | Reactions (T10) | Two rounds of three: E2, E4, E5, then E7, E9, E10 | F10 a card: you can't come to a friend's wedding |
| R6 | Opinion (T13) | K3 not eating meat; K9 everyone should have a pet; [Plan's own] *Påstående: Barn borde hjälpa till hemma varje dag. Håller du med?* | K8 letter: own house or flat |
| R7 | Tell and describe (T14, T15) | L2 a day you never forget; L4 your job or studies; L10 your first winter in Finland | L3 a friend will stay in your flat: describe it |
| R8 | Event, invitation (T6, T7) | H2 invite Sara to dinner; H5 accept a party invitation and ask details; H8 ask a colleague to a lunch walk | H9 invitation to your child's birthday party |

**Why it matters for the exam:** A function that failed in a mock will fail again on test day unless it is drilled on new cards. Missing required points is the error the official sample tasks make easiest to see [Official sample].

**How we learn it (60 min):**
- 0–8 Review: cards from Days 28, 44, 51, 55 and 57.
- 8–15 Choose the function; say its template twice.
- 15–33 The 3 speaking prompts, back to back, recorded. Timings from 2.1 and 6.2: calls 6 turns × 20 s [Plan's practice limit]; voicemail 20 s [Official sample]; Berätta and opinion 1 min prep + 1.5 min; reactions 20 s prep + 25 s [Teacher, unofficial]. Listen once for points; redo the weakest once.
- 33–51 The writing prompt (15 min; 18 min for a letter [Plan's own]), checked in the 3.4 order.
- 51–57 Notice routine (7.4), shortened.
- 57–60 Error log. Mark the 4 prompts as used.

**Speaking output:** the row's 3 prompts, with the section's default points from the appendix plus each prompt's extra points.

**Writing output:** the row's writing prompt; formal texts also need a formal opening, a formal closing and contact details.

**Listening and reading:** notice routine: what, when, where, for whom, cost or condition.

**Done when:** all 4 tasks are done; at least 3 of 4 cover every point; every task is inside its limit; in the redo, the function's top error type does not appear.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- Skipping a card's extra point, such as the price question in A1 or the counter-offer in B9.
- *Jag har väntat sedan en timme.* → *Jag har väntat i en timme.* (G9)
- *Krya på dig!* to news of a death (E2) → *Jag beklagar sorgen.* (reactions 8 and 12 fit different news)

### Day 59: weakest function B (Phase 3, week 9)

**What we learn today:** the same routine as Day 58 for the next weakest function from Mock 2, using its row in the Day 58 menu (seen practice). Add one check: if today's errors take any grammar error type to 3 or more this week, that type is red (3.6). Do a 10-sentence drill on it today and every day until you have 2 clean days.

**Why it matters for the exam:** The top five functions cover about 86% of one practice book's dialogue and reaction tasks [Book count]. Two weak functions fixed before the Phase 3 exit test are worth more than one function polished twice.

**How we learn it (60 min):**
- 0–8 Review: cards from Days 29, 45, 52, 56 and 58.
- 8–12 Template of today's function, aloud twice.
- 12–17 Red-error drill, if you have one (10 sentences); otherwise say the reasons or reactions that fit today's row.
- 17–35 The row's 3 speaking prompts, recorded, listened to once for points, the weakest redone once.
- 35–53 The row's writing prompt (15 min, or 18 for a letter).
- 53–57 Easy-news routine (7.3), shortened.
- 57–60 Error log; mark the 4 prompts as used.

**Speaking output:** the row's 3 prompts at the Day 58 timings.

**Writing output:** the row's writing prompt.

**Listening and reading:** easy-news routine: 3 facts, then every number, date, name and place.

**Done when:** as Day 58; and the red-error drill, if any, has 10/10 correct sentences.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- The same memorised line in all 3 tasks, such as *Det är bråttom* (It's urgent) when nothing is urgent. Delete what the card does not ask for.
- *Jag undrar om kan jag få en ny tid.* → *Jag undrar om jag kan få en ny tid.* (G3)
- In a seller call, agreeing to the first low offer. T3 gives a counter-offer: *Tyvärr kan jag inte gå ner så mycket, men du kan få den för [130] euro.*

- `p74-OC`: p. 74, Din åsikt C, T13: Healthcare here: pros, cons, staffing, comparison with your home country

### Day 60: formal writing set (Phase 3, week 9)

**What we learn today:**
- Three formal templates at speed: T2 (*Jag skriver för att fråga om [___]. Jag undrar om [___]. Tack på förhand.*), T5 (*Jag skriver om faktura nummer [___] från den [datum]. Fakturan är för hög av två skäl.*) and T12 (*Jag vill anmäla att [___]. Det hände [___]. Kan ni [___]?*).
- The formal frame for all three: *Hej,* or *Bästa mottagare,* … *Ni når mig på [telefonnummer] eller [e-post].* *Med vänliga hälsningar, [för- och efternamn]*.
- One phone refusal (T8): *Tack, men jag behöver inget nytt abonnemang.* (Thanks, but I don't need a new subscription.)

**Why it matters for the exam:** One of the three writing sample tasks is formal: a complaint with which bill, at least two reasons, a suitable amount and contact details [Official sample]. Writing three formal texts in a row trains the points-first habit under time.

**How we learn it (60 min):**
- 0–6 Review: cards from Days 30, 46, 53, 57 and 59.
- 6–11 The call (seen practice), 4 turns × 20 s [Plan's practice limit], recorded.
- 11–50 Three formal texts, 13 minutes each [Plan's own: shorter than the 20-minute mock limit, to build speed]. Write each task's points as tick boxes first.
- 50–56 Notice routine with an official letter, shortened.
- 56–60 Error log.

**Speaking output (seen practice):** G3: a salesperson phones and offers you a new mobile subscription; you don't want it. Points (4): acknowledge or thank; a clear no; a reason; a kind close.

**Writing output (seen practice):**
- T2, A8: write to your child's school; ask when the parents' meeting is and whether there is information in English. Points (6): formal opening; who you are (whose parent); question 1; question 2; contact details; formal closing.
- T5, D4: a removal firm damaged your table and charged more hours than agreed. Points (7): which bill (number, date, amount); reason 1, the damage; reason 2, the hours; a suitable amount; what you want; contact details; formal opening and closing.
- T12, J6: a neighbour has left rubbish in the stairwell for several days; write to the landlord. Points (6): who you are and your flat; what; since when; why it is a problem; what you want; contact details with a formal closing.

Model (J6): *Hej! Jag bor i lägenhet B 12 på Solvägen 5. Det har legat sopsäckar i trapphuset sedan i måndags. Det luktar illa, och barnen leker där. Kan ni ta bort soporna och prata med de boende? Ni når mig på 040 123 4567. Med vänliga hälsningar, Alex Kumar* (*en sopsäck* = a rubbish bag; *de boende* = the residents.)

**Listening and reading:** notice routine on a letter from a housing company or the city.

**Done when:** each text was written in 13 minutes and covers all its points with at most 2 engine errors (the Phase 3 exit limit); the call has 4/4 points.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- *Jag undrar när är föräldramötet.* → *Jag undrar när föräldramötet är.* (G3)
- *Soporna har legat där sedan tre dagar.* → *Soporna har legat där i tre dagar.* (G9)
- A bill complaint with no amount. The official sample requires one.

[Unreviewed Swedish: have a teacher check it]

**Book practice (*Förbered dig för allmän språkexamen*, pointers only; descriptions are our own words):** 2 task(s) today. Each replaces one of today's own prompts, or the redo of one; if nothing fits, it waits for a 90-minute day (see the note at the top of this file).
- `p38-S2`: p. 38, Skriva 2, T2: Email an estate agent: home type, location, budget, move date and reason
- `p38-S3`: p. 38, Skriva 3, T5: Complaint to the housing company board about building faults, with deadline and consequence

### Day 61: speaking set (Phase 3, week 9)

**What we learn today:**
- Four first lines, so no task starts with silence: T14 *Jag vill berätta om [___].*; T3 seller *Ja, den finns kvar.*; T4 *Jag ringer angående [___]. Den är fortfarande trasig.*; T9 *Hej [namn], det är [namn]. Förlåt, jag blir [en kvart] sen, eftersom [___].*
- *en barnvagn* (a pram) is an en-word, so *den*: *Den är två år gammal.* (G7)
- Negative duration takes *på*: *Hissen har inte fungerat på en vecka.* (The lift hasn't worked for a week.) This is the wording of appendix C3; teacher to confirm.

**Why it matters for the exam:** The speaking sample has a person talk, a seller call, a repair call and a 20-second voicemail [Official sample]. Running four types back to back is the rehearsal for a full set.

**How we learn it (60 min):**
- 0–6 Review: cards from Days 31, 47, 54, 58 and 60.
- 6–9 The four first lines aloud.
- 9–24 Speaking set, back to back, recorded (seen practice, below).
- 24–32 Listen to each task once for points; listen to the weakest a second time for clarity.
- 32–47 Writing (15 min).
- 47–55 Easy-news routine, shortened.
- 55–60 Error log. Send this week's teacher pack today (2 newest recordings, 2 newest texts); the Day 62–63 exit tasks go in the next pack.

**Speaking output (seen practice):**
- a) L7, Berätta, 1 min prep + 1.5 min [Official sample timing]: a person who has helped you. Use the six-point talk plan (Day 71).
- b) B1, seller, 6 turns × 20 s: you sell a pram. Points (6): still available; age; condition; price; an answer to a lower offer; a time and your address.
- c) C8, complaint, 6 turns × 20 s: the lift in your building has been broken for a week; call the landlord. Points (7): who; which building and flat; the problem; since when; why it is urgent for you; what you want and by when; a close.
- d) Voicemail, 20 s [Plan's own]: *Du ska skjutsa din kollega Jonna till jobbet klockan sju, men du har försovit dig. Lämna ett meddelande.* (You're driving your colleague to work at seven, but you overslept.) Points (4): who; the apology; the reason; a new time or another plan.

Model (d): *Hej Jonna, det är Alex. Förlåt, jag har försovit mig! Jag kan vara hos dig kvart över sju. Om du inte kan vänta, ta bussen, så ses vi på jobbet.* (about 30 words)

**Writing output:** H3: invite your colleagues to a farewell coffee (*en avskedsfika*). 15 min. Points (7): opening; what; when; where; why; a reply-by date; closing. About 50–80 words [Teacher, unofficial].

**Listening and reading:** easy-news routine.

**Done when:** all 4 speaking tasks are inside their limits with all their points; the talk lasts at least 1 minute (aim 1.5) with no more than 2 pauses over 5 seconds; the invitation has 7/7 points.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- *Hissen fungerar inte sedan en vecka.* → *Hissen har inte fungerat på en vecka.* (G6)
- *Det är två år gammal* about the pram → *Den är två år gammal.* (G7)
- *Om du kan inte vänta …* → *Om du inte kan vänta …* (G2)

[Unreviewed Swedish: have a teacher check it]

### Day 62: Phase 3 exit test, part 1 (Phase 3, week 9)

**What we learn today:** nothing new. Five of the ten exit prompts (section 4.4; seen practice, so the helper changes their details). The ten together come from eight topic areas and include two less common functions (I3 = T11 advice, J5 = T12 report), as 4.4 requires.

**Why it matters for the exam:** The real prompts are unseen, every instruction is in Swedish [Official], and the topics come from nine fixed areas [Official]. This test shows whether the templates transfer to topics you have not drilled.

**How we learn it (about 60 min):**
- 0–5 Review: a light pass over cards from Days 32, 48, 55, 59 and 61.
- 5–8 Set up: timer, recorder, cards face down, paper for preparation only.
- 8–20 Tasks 1–3, back to back, recorded. About 15 s to read each card [Teacher, unofficial].
- 20–35 Task 5, informal (15 min).
- 35–55 Task 4, formal (20 min). Both typed, spelling help off, no dictionary.
- 55–60 Note the time of each task. Do not mark anything yet.

**Speaking output (seen practice):**
1. C6 (home and housing), call, 6 turns × 20 s: a neighbour plays loud music every night; call the landlord. Extra points: your flat; how it affects you.
2. G10 (work), 20 s prep + 25 s [Teacher, unofficial]: a colleague wants you to give a speech at the party; you don't want to. Points: thanks; a clear no; a reason; an alternative or a kind close.
3. I3 (health and wellbeing, T11), 30 s prep + 1 min [Plan's own]: a colleague sleeps badly; give advice. Points: at least 2 pieces of advice, each with a reason or example; a friendly close.

**Writing output (seen practice):**
4. D3 (shops and services), 20 min [Plan's own]: a jacket bought online; the zip broke after two weeks. Points: when you bought it (order number); what is wrong; what you want; contact details; formal opening and closing.
5. H7 (culture), 15 min: a language café where you can speak Swedish. Points: what; when; where; two more details; an invitation question; opening and closing.

**Listening and reading:** none today (test day).

**Done when:** all 5 tasks are attempted, none left blank, and each finished time is written down. The pass rule is checked on Day 63.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- In C6, no flat number. The landlord needs to know who is calling.
- In I3, one piece of advice only. The default points ask for at least 2.
- *Dragkedjan gick sönder efter två veckor, så jag vill har en ny.* → *… så jag vill ha en ny.* (G4)

[Unreviewed Swedish: have a teacher check it]

### Day 63: Phase 3 exit test, part 2 (Phase 3, week 9)

**What we learn today:** the other five exit prompts, then the count of all ten.

**Why it matters for the exam:** Phase 4 is timed full sets and mocks. Starting it with a function or topic that still fails only repeats that failure under time pressure.

**How we learn it (about 75 min):**
- 0–3 Set up as on Day 62.
- 3–15 Tasks 6–8, back to back, recorded.
- 15–40 Task 9, letter (25 min [Plan's own], about 100–150 words [Teacher, unofficial]).
- 40–60 Task 10, formal (20 min).
- 60–65 Count all ten tasks (6.3): points per task, time, engine errors per text. Put all ten in the teacher pack.
- 65–75 All overdue cards.

**Speaking output (seen practice):**
6. L5 (me and my background), 1 min prep + 1.5 min: a holiday or festival (*en högtid*) in your home country.
7. J5 (society, T12), call, 6 turns × 20 s: the street lamp outside your house does not work; call the municipality. Points: who; what; where (address); since when; why it is a problem; what you want; contact details.
8. K5 (travel), 1 min prep + 1.5 min: *Det är bättre att resa med tåg än med flyg.* Points: opinion; 2 reasons; an example; the other view; a matching conclusion.

**Writing output (seen practice):**
9. K6 (society), letter: all adults should learn a new language. Points (8): topic line; opinion; 3 reasons; an example; the other view; a matching conclusion.
10. A10 (shops and services): write to a restaurant; book a table for eight on Saturday and ask about vegetarian food. Points: day; time; number of people; the vegetarian question; contact details; formal opening and closing.

**Listening and reading:** one clip after the count, if time allows.

**Done when** (section 4.4):
- Countable: at least 9 of the 10 tasks cover all their points; at least 9 of the 10 finish in time; no more than 2 engine errors in each written text.
- Teacher: at least 8 of the 10 rated A or B, and none D. With no outside rater, the pass is provisional (3.5).
- **If it fails:** add one extra week of topic days for the failing topics or functions (repeat the matching Days 43–61 with new prompts), then retest. Do not start Day 64 before the pass.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- *På Diwali vi tänder ljus.* → *På Diwali tänder vi ljus.* (G1)
- *Jag undrar om har ni vegetarisk mat.* → *Jag undrar om ni har vegetarisk mat.* (G3)
- In J5, no address. The card's "where" is a required point.

[Unreviewed Swedish: have a teacher check it]

### Day 64: full speaking set A (Phase 4, week 10)

**What we learn today:** no new content. Phase 4 starts only if the Phase 3 exit test passed (4.5). Today is the first full speaking set: the four official-sample formats plus three reactions, timed, back to back. It is also the first study day of the week, so it starts with the top-3 error drill (6.1).

**Why it matters for the exam:** The speaking sample is a person talk, a seller call, a repair call and a 20-second voicemail [Official sample], with reactions as a further practice format [Teacher, unofficial]. Running them without breaks builds the stamina a long test day needs (about 5 hours with breaks [Official]).

**How we learn it (60 min):**
- 0–6 Review: cards from Days 34, 50, 57, 61 and 63.
- 6–11 Top-3 error drill: last week's 3 most frequent error types, a few new sentences each (3.6).
- 11–14 First lines of T1, T3, T4, T9, T10 and T14 aloud.
- 14–34 Set A (seen practice), recorded, no pauses between tasks.
- 34–50 Listen to each task twice (points, then clarity) and tick its checklist. No writing task today.
- 50–57 Easy-news routine, shortened.
- 57–60 Error log.

**Speaking output (seen practice):**
- S1, Berätta, 1 min prep + 1.5 min [Plan's own prompt]: *Berätta om ditt hem. Var bor du, hur ser det ut och vad tycker du om det?* (Tell about your home.) Points (6): where; what kind of home and rooms; what it looks like; the area; what you like, with a reason; a wish.
- S2, seller, 6 turns × 20 s: B6, you sell a winter jacket. The voice: 1 *Hej, jag ringer om vinterjackan i annonsen. Finns den kvar?* 2 *Vilken storlek och färg är den?* 3 *Är den varm?* 4 *Vad kostar den?* 5 *Kan du ta 30 euro?* 6 *Var kan vi träffas?* Points (6): still available; size and colour; condition; price; an answer to the offer; a meeting place and time.
- S3, repair call, 6 turns × 20 s [Plan's own card]: *Din dammsugare har varit på reparation, men den slutar fortfarande fungera efter fem minuter. Ring butiken. Förklara problemet, protestera och säg vad du vill.* (Your vacuum cleaner was repaired but still stops after five minutes.) The voice: 1 *Kundtjänst, hej.* 2 *Vad gäller det?* 3 *När var den på reparation?* 4 *Du kan lämna in den igen nästa vecka.* 5 *Tyvärr kan vi inte ge dig en ny.* 6 *Jag ska fråga min chef. Vi ringer dig på fredag.* Points (6): who; what it concerns; the fault; that it was already repaired, and when; a protest with a reason; what you want, with a time limit.
- S4, voicemail, 20 s [Plan's own]: *Du ska spela badminton med din kollega Mikko klockan sju, men du måste jobba över. Lämna ett meddelande och föreslå en ny dag.* Points (4): who; the apology; the reason; a new day.
- S5, three reactions, 20 s prep + 25 s each [Plan's own]: a) *Din vän berättar att hon har vunnit en resa till Rom.* (Your friend has won a trip to Rome.) b) *Din granne berättar att någon har brutit sig in i hennes bil.* (Someone broke into your neighbour's car.) c) *En kollega bjuder in dig till sin födelsedagsfest på lördag. Du vill gärna komma.* (Accept.) Points per reaction (3): a reaction that fits; a detail or question; an offer or a close.

**Writing output:** none (the recordings are reviewed instead).

**Listening and reading:** easy-news routine.

**Done when:** every task is inside its limit; every task covers all its points; S1 lasts at least 1 minute 15 seconds with no more than 2 pauses over 5 seconds [Plan's own]; the checklists are ticked for all 7 recordings.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- *Jag tycker om min lägenhet eftersom den är inte dyr.* → *… eftersom den inte är dyr.* (G2)
- *Den slutar fungera fortfarande.* → *Den slutar fortfarande fungera.* (G2: *fortfarande* goes where *inte* goes)
- In S5 c), answering an invitation with *Tack!* only. Accept and ask: *Ja, gärna! Vad ska jag ta med?*

[Unreviewed Swedish: have a teacher check it]

### Day 65: full writing set A (Phase 4, week 10)

**What we learn today:** no new content. The three writing formats at the mock limits: W1 informal 15 min, W2 letter 25 min, W3 formal 20 min [Plan's own limits], typed, spelling help off.

**Why it matters for the exam:** The writing sample has an informal event e-mail, a letter to the editor with at least three reasons, and a bill complaint [Official sample]. Writing all three in one hour is the hardest stamina test in the plan.

**How we learn it (about 80 min):**
- 0–4 Review: cards from Days 35, 51, 58, 62 and 64 (short pass).
- 4–19 W1. Points as tick boxes first, then write.
- 19–44 W2.
- 44–64 W3.
- 64–72 Count (6.3): points per task, word counts, engine errors per text.
- 72–78 Notice routine, shortened.
- 78–80 Error log. **Send the teacher pack today: all of set A (Day 64 recordings and today's three texts)**, so that the feedback is back for Day 67.
- With only 60 minutes: do W2 and W3 today, and W1 first thing tomorrow.

**Writing output (seen practice):**
- W1 [Plan's own]: *Skriv till din vän om en gratis yogakurs i parken. Skriv vad, när och var, och ge två andra detaljer. Fråga om din vän vill följa med.* Points (8): opening; what; when; where; detail 1; detail 2; invitation question; closing. About 50–80 words [Teacher, unofficial].
- W2, K10: letter, school should start later in the morning. Points (8): topic line; opinion; reason 1; reason 2; reason 3; an example; the other view; a matching conclusion. About 100–150 words. Reasons that fit: 4, 12, 16 for; 35 and 36 against.
- W3, D2: the electricity company's bill is twice as high as usual. Points (8): which bill (number, date, amount); your normal amount; reason 1; reason 2; a suitable amount; what you want; contact details; formal opening and closing. The amounts must add up.

Model (W3): *Hej! Jag skriver om faktura nummer 55120 från den 4 februari. Fakturan är på 180 euro, men jag brukar betala ungefär 90 euro i månaden. Jag tycker att fakturan är för hög av två skäl. För det första var jag bortrest i två veckor. För det andra har vi inte köpt några nya maskiner. Jag tycker att 90 euro är ett rimligt belopp. Kan ni skicka en ny faktura? Ni når mig på 040 123 4567. Med vänliga hälsningar, Alex Kumar* (about 80 words)

**Listening and reading:** notice routine.

**Done when:** each task is finished inside its limit; W1 has 8/8, W2 8/8 and W3 8/8 points; each text has at most 2 engine errors; W2 has 100–150 words.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- *För det första jag var bortrest.* → *För det första var jag bortrest.* (G1)
- W2 with a conclusion that does not match the opening (step 5 must agree with step 1).
- *Jag betalar 90 euro i månad.* → *… i månaden.*

### Day 66: listening and reading set (Phase 4, week 10)

**What we learn today:**
- Numbers at speed: the 2.7 traps (*femton/femtio, arton/åttio, halv tre* = 2:30, *kvart i tio* = 9:45), prices, dates and phone numbers digit by digit.
- The 14 meaning-flip words in 2.6 (*inte, aldrig, ingen, men, utan, i stället, tyvärr, redan, fortfarande, inställd, flyttad, ändrad, stängt, försenad*).
- One check line for calls: repeat a number back. *Den tolfte januari klockan sex, alltså?* (The twelfth of January at six, then?)

**Why it matters for the exam:** The listening and reading samples use multiple choice, true/false and short open answers [Official sample], and numbers are the cheapest points (7.1). The phone tasks also start with understanding a recorded voice.

**How we learn it (60 min):**
- 0–5 Review: cards from Days 36, 52, 59, 63 and 65.
- 5–11 Numbers and flip words: the helper reads 20 number items (write digits) and 6 sentences with a flip word; say what changed.
- 11–17 The call, recorded.
- 17–29 The informal task (12 min [Plan's own]).
- 29–53 Timed set: 2 new easy-news clips (about 7 min each: read the questions, listen, answer, listen again, check) and 2 new notices (5 min each).
- 53–57 Score each item as a percentage.
- 57–60 Error log; enter the scores in the progress record.

**Speaking output:** call, 6 turns × 20 s [Plan's own card]: *Ring medborgarinstitutet. Du vill gå en kurs i finska för nybörjare. Fråga när kursen börjar, vad den kostar och hur man anmäler sig.* The voice: 1 *Medborgarinstitutet, god morgon.* 2 *Ja, vi har en kurs för nybörjare. Vad vill du veta?* 3 *Den börjar tisdagen den 12 januari klockan 18.* 4 *Den kostar 85 euro för tolv gånger.* 5 *Man anmäler sig på nätet före den 5 januari.* 6 *Varsågod. Hej då!* Points (6): who and why; when it starts; the price; how to sign up; one number repeated back; a close. (*medborgarinstitut*: an adult education centre; ask the teacher which name is used near you.)

**Writing output:** I6: a friend will visit Finland in winter; write what to pack. Points (4): opening; at least 3 things to pack, each with a reason; one tip; closing.

**Listening and reading:** the timed set. The helper writes about 8 questions per clip and per notice in the three formats (for example 3 multiple choice, 3 true/false, 2 short open answers [Plan's own split]) without showing you the text. With no helper, an AI can write the questions from the transcript; count those scores as training only, because only teacher- or helper-written questions count for the 70% rule (6.2).

**Done when:** at least 18/20 number items; all 6 flip sentences understood; the call has 6/6 points; each listening and reading item is scored, with the percentage in the progress record.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- Writing 15 for *femtio*. Say the pair aloud once before every clip.
- Missing *inte* in *Kursen börjar inte i januari*. Check every true/false item for a flip word.
- An open answer as a long sentence. Write 1–4 words you heard (7.5).

[Unreviewed Swedish: have a teacher check it]

### Day 67: teacher feedback day (Phase 4, week 10)

**What we learn today:** the fix routine (3.6) applied to set A. Turn the teacher's sheets and your error log into 3 targeted drills, then redo the weakest speaking task and the weakest writing task of set A with one detail changed. With no feedback back yet, use your own count and mark today's result provisional.

**How to build the 3 drills:**
1. List every error from set A in the log by type (G1–G10, word choice, missing point, over time), adding the teacher's top 3 per task.
2. Take the 3 types with the highest "times seen". A type seen 3 or more times this week is red.
3. Make one 5-minute drill per type: a word-order type → 10 new sentences of that pattern on this week's topics; a missing point → 5 old cards, points as tick boxes, then one sentence per point only; over time → cut each answer to the 30-word voicemail guide or 2 sentences per call turn, then re-time it.

**Worked example (G2, *inte* in a subordinate clause).** Log lines: Day 64 S1 *eftersom den är inte dyr*; Day 65 W3 *att vi har inte köpt nya maskiner*; Day 66 call *om jag kan inte komma*. Seen 3 times: red. Drill, 10 sentences, *inte* before the verb after *eftersom, att, om, som*:
1. *Jag ringer eftersom dammsugaren inte fungerar.*
2. *Jag skriver eftersom jag inte har fått pengarna tillbaka.*
3. *Problemet är att fakturan inte stämmer.* (*stämma* = be correct)
4. *Jag hoppas att det inte tar lång tid.*
5. *Jag vet att ni inte har tid i dag.*
Then 5 more of your own; the teacher checks them in the next pack.

**Why it matters for the exam:** "Basic structures often wrong" is a level-2 sign [Official]. Fixing the three most frequent errors removes more mistakes than any new content.

**How we learn it (60 min):**
- 0–6 Review: cards from Days 37, 53, 60, 64 and 66.
- 6–10 Read the sheets; count; pick the top 3.
- 10–25 The 3 drills, 5 minutes each.
- 25–37 Redo the weakest speaking task of set A with one new detail. Record; compare with Day 64.
- 37–52 Redo the weakest writing task with one new detail (15 min).
- 52–57 Easy-news routine, shortened.
- 57–60 Error log; error cards for at most 3 of the drilled sentences (3.3).

**Speaking output:** the weakest set A task again, for example S3 with a coffee machine instead of a vacuum cleaner.

**Writing output:** the weakest set A text again, for example W3 with a water bill instead of electricity.

**Listening and reading:** easy-news routine.

**Done when:** 3 drills done with all sentences correct; both redos cover every point; the drilled error types do not appear in the redos.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- Redoing the best task instead of the weakest. The rating sheet decides.
- Drilling 10 sentences that copy one model. Change the subject and the verb each time.
- *Jag hoppas att det blir inte dyrt.* → *Jag hoppas att det inte blir dyrt.* (G2)

[Unreviewed Swedish: have a teacher check it]

And the adapt rule from Day 48: underline the points, pick the template, fill every slot, delete what is not asked.

Points per reaction (3).

### Day 72: fix day (Phase 4, week 11)

**What we learn today:** the fix routine (Day 67) on set B and this week's log: count the error types, take the top 3, make 3 five-minute drills, then redo 2 speaking tasks and 1 writing task with new details.

**Worked example (missing point).** Log lines: Day 71 b) seller call, no address (the card says *säg var du bor*); Day 71 event message, no "where"; Day 68 C10, only one of the two problems. Type "missing point", seen 3 times: red. Drill (5 minutes):
1. Take 5 old cards (Days 54 a, 61 b, 64 S2, 50 writing, 65 W1).
2. For each, write the points as tick boxes in 30 seconds.
3. Say or type **one sentence per point** and nothing else, about 1 minute per card.
4. Add a capital-letter line to the top of the template card (3.6, rule 4). T3: SÄG VAR DU BOR: *Jag bor på [Solvägen 5] i [Esbo].* T6: SKRIV VAR: *Det är [i parken vid stationen].*

The other two drills follow the Day 67 recipe for their type.

**Why it matters for the exam:** The sample tasks list their required points [Official sample], and a missing point is the one error no grammar can repair. Point-checking is a habit, so it is drilled like grammar.

**How we learn it (60 min):**
- 0–6 Review: cards from Days 42, 58, 65, 69 and 71.
- 6–10 Read the teacher's set B sheets (or count yourself: provisional); pick the top 3.
- 10–25 The 3 drills.
- 25–39 Redo the 2 weakest set B speaking tasks with new details. Record; tick the points.
- 39–52 Redo 1 writing task: the Day 71 event message or the Day 69 letter, whichever scored lower (13 min [Plan's own]).
- 52–57 Notice routine, shortened.
- 57–60 Error log.

**Speaking output:** for example set B c) with a broken phone instead of a computer, and d) with a new reason and time.

**Writing output:** the lower-scoring text, with a new event or a new statement.

**Listening and reading:** notice routine.

**Done when:** 3 drills done with every sentence correct; both speaking redos and the writing redo cover every point; no drilled error type appears in the redos.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- *Jag bor i Solvägen 5.* → *Jag bor på Solvägen 5.* (a street address takes *på*, as in T3)
- *Du kan komma i morgon efter sex klockan.* → *… efter klockan sex.*
- Redoing a task with the same details as before. Change at least one; otherwise you are memorising one answer (3.4).

### Day 73: full writing set B (Phase 4, week 11)

**What we learn today:** no new content. Three reactions to warm up, then writing set B at the mock limits: W1 15 min, W2 25 min, W3 20 min [Plan's own limits].

**Why it matters for the exam:** All three writing sample types in one sitting [Official sample]; this second full set shows whether set A's errors have gone.

**How we learn it (about 80 min):**
- 0–4 Review: cards from Days 43, 59, 66, 70 and 72 (short pass).
- 4–8 Three reactions (seen practice), recorded.
- 8–23 W1. 23–48 W2. 48–68 W3. Points as tick boxes before each.
- 68–74 Count: points, words, engine errors per text.
- 74–80 Easy-news routine, shortened; error log.

**Speaking output (seen practice):** reactions, 20 s prep + 25 s each [Plan's own]: a) *Din chef säger att du har gjort ett mycket bra jobb i år.* (Your manager praises your work.) b) *Din kollega berättar att hon inte fick lägenheten som hon ville ha.* (Your colleague didn't get the flat she wanted.) c) *En granne ber om ursäkt för att hans hund skällde hela natten.* (A neighbour apologises because his dog barked all night.) Points per reaction (3).

**Writing output (seen practice):**
- W1 [Plan's own]: *Din vän vill träffas i helgen. Skriv och föreslå en utflykt till en nationalpark. Skriv när och var ni träffas, hur ni åker dit och vad ni ska ta med.* Points (7): opening; the trip; when; where you meet; how you travel; what to bring; closing with a question.
- W2 [Plan's own]: *Skriv en insändare: Det är bättre att resa i sitt eget land än utomlands. Skriv din åsikt och ge minst tre skäl.* Points (8), as on Day 65.
- W3, D8: the dentist has billed you for treatment you did not get. Points (7): which bill (number, date, amount); reason 1; reason 2; a suitable amount (0 euro, or the part you accept); what you want (a corrected bill); contact details; formal opening and closing.

Model (W1): *Hej Sara! Vill du följa med på en utflykt till en nationalpark på lördag? Vi kan träffas vid järnvägsstationen klockan nio. Vi åker buss dit, och resan tar ungefär en timme. Där kan vi gå fem kilometer och grilla korv. Ta med varma kläder, bra skor och något att dricka. Vad säger du? Skriv till mig före torsdag! Kram, Alex* (about 65 words)

**Listening and reading:** easy-news routine.

**Done when:** every task inside its limit; W1 7/7, W2 8/8 (100–150 words), W3 7/7 points; at most 2 engine errors per text; the reactions fit the news.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- *Vi kan träffa vid stationen.* → *Vi kan träffas vid stationen.* (*träffas* = meet each other)
- *… behandlingen som jag har inte fått.* → *… behandlingen som jag inte har fått.* (G2)
- In b), *Grattis!* by reflex. Bad news needs reaction 6 or 7: *Åh nej, vad synd!*

### Day 74: outside functions (Phase 4, week 11)

**What we learn today:**
- T16 directions, as a chain: *Gå ut från huset och ta till vänster.* (Go out of the building and turn left.) *Gå rakt fram i ungefär hundra meter.* (Go straight on for about 100 metres.) *Ta sedan till höger vid busshållplatsen.* (Then turn right at the bus stop.) *Affären ligger mittemot skolan.* (The shop is opposite the school.) *Det tar fem minuter att gå.* (It takes five minutes to walk.)
- T11 advice: *Jag tycker att du ska [___]. Om jag var du skulle jag [___]. Det är en bra idé att [___].*
- T12 report: *Jag vill anmäla att [en bil har stått framför porten i tre dagar].* (*en port* = the front door of a building.)

**Why it matters for the exam:** In one practice book, 8 of 21 writing tasks fall outside the top five functions [Book count]. These moves stop an unusual card from becoming a blank answer.

**How we learn it (60 min):**
- 0–6 Review: cards from Days 44, 60, 67, 71 and 73.
- 6–15 The three line sets aloud; a ladder (3.2) on the directions chain with your own street.
- 15–30 Two speaking prompts (seen practice), recorded; listen once; redo the weaker.
- 30–45 The writing prompt (15 min).
- 45–53 Notice routine.
- 53–60 Error log; at most 8 new cards (3.3).

**Speaking output (seen practice):**
- L8 (T16), 30 s prep + 1 min [Plan's own]: describe the way from your home to the nearest shop. Points (5): the start; at least 3 direction steps; a landmark; how long it takes; a close.
- J9 (T12), call, 6 turns × 20 s: a car has been parked in front of your building's door for three days; call the landlord. Points: who and where you live; what; since when; why it is a problem; what you want; contact details.

**Writing output (seen practice):** I8 (T11): a friend wants to save money; write three suggestions. Points (5): opening; 3 suggestions, each with a reason or an example; closing.

**Listening and reading:** notice routine.

**Done when:** the directions have 5/5 points inside 1 minute; the call has all its points; the advice e-mail has 5/5 points and at most 2 engine errors.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- *Om jag var du jag skulle laga mat hemma.* → *Om jag var du skulle jag laga mat hemma.* (G1)
- *Bilen står där sedan tre dagar.* → *Bilen har stått där i tre dagar.* (G6, G9)
- Directions with no landmark. One fixed point (*banken, skolan, busshållplatsen*) makes them usable.

### Day 75: listening and reading set (Phase 4, week 11)

**What we learn today:**
- A retest of the instruction words: the 13 in 2.6 (*läs, lyssna, välj, skriv, svara, berätta, förklara, beskriv, jämför, föreslå, motivera, rätt/fel, åtminstone*) plus 20 question and meaning-flip words. Pass marks: 13/13 and 18/20, as in the Phase 1 exit (E1).
- *minst* and *åtminstone* both mean "at least"; *högst* means "at most" (one card).

**Why it matters for the exam:** Every instruction is in Swedish [Official]; a misread instruction costs points before your Swedish is tested (2.6). This is the second timed listening and reading set, so its score can be compared with Day 66.

**How we learn it (60 min):**
- 0–5 Review: cards from Days 45, 61, 68, 72 and 74.
- 5–12 The word test: the helper shows each word once; write the English.
- 12–18 The call (seen practice), recorded.
- 18–48 Timed set: 2 new clips and 2 new notices, as on Day 66, with about 8 helper-written questions per item in the three formats.
- 48–54 Score; compare with Day 66 in the progress record.
- 54–60 Error log; cards for every missed word and number.

**Speaking output (seen practice):** J3 (T12), call, 6 turns × 20 s: someone has stolen your bike; call the police. Points: who; what was stolen; when and where; a description with 2 details; what you want; contact details.

**Writing output:** none today.

**Listening and reading:** the timed set. As on Day 66, AI-written questions are training only; teacher- or helper-written ones count (6.2).

**Done when:** 13/13 and 18/20 on the word test; the call has all its points; the set is scored, with the percentage next to Day 66's.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- Reading *minst tre skäl* as "at most three". *minst* = at least.
- *Min cykel är stulen i går.* → *Någon stal min cykel i går.* (G6; a finished time takes the past)
- Changing an answer because one word was unknown. Answer from the whole sentence and move on (7.5).

### Day 76: weakest tasks (Phase 4, week 11)

**What we learn today:** your own weak spots from the error log, not new content.
- List every task of sets A and B and Days 68–75 with its share of points and its rating. Pick the 2 speaking types and 1 writing type with the lowest results.
- The helper gives a matching unused prompt from the Day 58 menu (seen practice). If that row is used up, the teacher or helper writes a new card of the same type (4.6 allows teacher-written prompts).

**Worked example (over time).** Log line: Day 64 S4 voicemail, 31 seconds. Before (about 50 words): *Hej Mikko, det är Alex, och jag ringer för att säga att jag tyvärr inte kan komma och spela i kväll, eftersom min chef har bett mig att stanna kvar och jobba över, för vi har mycket att göra just nu, så …* After (about 25 words): *Hej Mikko, det är Alex. Förlåt, jag kan inte komma i kväll, eftersom jag måste jobba över. Kan vi spela på torsdag i stället? Hej!* The rule: who, apology, one short reason, the new plan. About 30 words fits 20 seconds (Day 84).

**Why it matters for the exam:** A turn cut off by the time limit loses its last point, and the voicemail sample is only 20 seconds [Official sample].

**How we learn it (60 min):**
- 0–6 Review: cards from Days 46, 62, 69, 73 and 75.
- 6–12 Make the list; pick the 3 tasks.
- 12–30 The 2 speaking tasks, recorded; listen once for points and time; redo once.
- 30–48 The writing task (15 min, or 18 for a letter).
- 48–56 Easy-news routine.
- 56–60 Error log.

**Speaking output:** the 2 weakest speaking types, on cards you have not practised (question-bank book tasks, or seen practice with the details changed).

**Writing output:** the weakest writing type, on a card you have not practised.

**Listening and reading:** easy-news routine.

**Done when:** all 3 tasks cover every point, inside their limits; each shows a better points share than the same type in set A or B.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- Cutting a voicemail by dropping who you are. Cut the reason instead.
- Picking the task you like, not the lowest one. The numbers decide (6.1).
- *Förlåt att jag är sen* in a voicemail left before the meeting → *Förlåt, jag blir lite sen.* (reaction 22 is for when you are already late; 23 is for before)

**What we learn today:** no new content. Set C is the full mock speaking set (6.2): S1–S6 back to back, about 30 minutes, on new prompts. S2 is a buyer call, because Mock 3 has a seller call and 6.2 trains both roles. First study day of week 12, so it starts with the top-3 error drill (6.1).

**Why it matters for the exam:** This is the last full speaking rehearsal before Mock 3, which is the first mock that counts towards booking (6.4).

**How we learn it (about 65 min):**
- 0–5 Review: cards from Days 48, 64, 71, 75 and 77 (short pass).
- 5–10 Top-3 error drill.
- 10–12 First lines of T1, T3, T4, T9, T10, T13 and T14.
- 12–42 Set C (seen practice), recorded, no pauses.
- 42–56 Listen to each task once and tick its checklist (points and time); listen to the two weakest a second time for clarity.

**Speaking output (seen practice):**
- S1, 1 min prep + 1.5 min [Plan's own]: *Berätta om en hobby som du har eller vill börja med.* (A hobby you have or want to start.) Points (6): what; since when or why; where and with whom; how often; why you like it; a plan or a wish.
- S2, buyer, 6 turns × 20 s: B4, a bike in an ad; you want to know if it suits an adult and when you can test-ride it. The voice: 1 *Hej, det är Johan.* 2 *Ja, cykeln finns kvar. Vad vill du veta?* 3 *Ja, den passar en vuxen. Den är ganska stor.* 4 *Den kostar 90 euro.* 5 *Hmm, jag kan ta 80 euro.* 6 *Du kan provcykla i morgon efter klockan fem.* Points (6): who and which ad; the size question; one more question; an offer; a test-ride time; a T1 confirmation.
- S3, complaint, 6 turns × 20 s: C5, you got the wrong item from an online shop; call customer service. The voice: 1 *Kundtjänst, hej.* 2 *Vad gäller det?* 3 *Har du ordernumret?* 4 *Vad beställde du, och vad fick du?* 5 *Du kan skicka tillbaka varan.* 6 *Okej. Vi skickar rätt vara i dag.* Points (6): who; the order number; what you ordered and what you got; a protest; who pays for the return; a close.
- S4, voicemail, 20 s [Plan's own]: *Du har en tid för att titta på en lägenhet klockan sex, men tåget är försenat. Lämna ett meddelande till hyresvärden.* Points (4): who; which viewing; the reason; a new time or a question.
- S5, three reactions, 20 s prep + 25 s each [Plan's own]: a) *Din vän berättar att han ska bli pappa.* b) *Din kollega har feber men har kommit till jobbet ändå.* c) *Du har lånat din kollegas paraply och glömt det på bussen. Säg förlåt.* Points per reaction (3).
- S6, 1 min prep + 1.5 min [Plan's own]: *Påstående: Det är bättre att köpa begagnade saker än nya. Håller du med? Motivera din åsikt.* Points (6).

**Writing output:** none (recordings reviewed instead).

**Listening and reading:** easy-news routine.

**Done when:** all 6 tasks inside their limits with every point covered; S1 and S6 each last at least 1 minute 15 seconds [Plan's own]; the set goes in the teacher pack with Day 79's texts.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- *Jag har beställt en blå tröja i måndags.* → *Jag beställde en blå tröja i måndags.* (G6)
- In S2, no offer. Use T3: *Kan du tänka dig att sälja den för [75] euro?*
- In b), *Krya på dig!* alone. Add the advice: *Du borde gå hem och vila.*

### Day 79: full writing set C (Phase 4, week 12)

**What we learn today:** no new content. Writing set C at the mock limits (W1 15, W2 25, W3 20 min [Plan's own]), typed, spelling help off.

**Why it matters for the exam:** The last full writing rehearsal before Mock 3; the three sample types again [Official sample].

- 0–4 Review: cards from Days 49, 65, 72, 76 and 78 (short pass).
- 4–19 W1. 19–44 W2. 44–64 W3.
- 64–72 Count: points, words, engine errors.

- 78–80 Error log. **Send the teacher pack today: set C speaking and writing**, so the feedback is back for Day 81.

**Writing output (seen practice):**
- W1, H6: invite your neighbours to a clean-up day in the yard. Points (7): opening (*Hej grannar!*); what; when; where; what people should do or bring; what happens afterwards; closing.
- W2 [Plan's own]: *Skriv en insändare: Det är bättre att läsa böcker på papper än på en skärm. Skriv din åsikt och ge minst tre skäl.* Points (8).
- W3, D10: you rented a car, and the firm wants you to pay for damage that was there before. Points (7): which rental (dates, contract number, amount); reason 1 (for example a photo); reason 2 (for example a witness or the pick-up form); a suitable amount (0 euro for the damage); what you want; contact details; formal opening and closing.

Model (W3): *Bästa mottagare, Jag hyrde en bil av er från den 10 till den 12 mars, avtal nummer 4471. Ni vill att jag betalar 300 euro för en skada på bakdörren. För det första fanns skadan redan när jag hämtade bilen. Jag har ett foto från den 10 mars. För det andra står skadan på papperet som jag fick. Därför är 0 euro ett rimligt belopp. Kan ni ta bort avgiften? Ni når mig på 040 123 4567. Med vänliga hälsningar, Alex Kumar* (about 80 words)

**Listening and reading:** notice routine.

**Done when:** every task inside its limit; W1 7/7, W2 8/8 (100–150 words), W3 7/7; at most 2 engine errors per text.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- *För det första skadan fanns redan.* → *För det första fanns skadan redan.* (G1)
- *Jag tycker att jag ska inte betala.* → *Jag tycker inte att jag ska betala.* (or *… att jag inte ska betala*; G2)
- *Efteråt vi dricker kaffe.* → *Efteråt dricker vi kaffe.* (G1)

### Day 81: fix day (Phase 4, week 12)

**What we learn today:** the fix routine (Day 67) on set C and Day 80, then redo 2 speaking tasks and 1 writing task from set C with new details. These are the last errors to remove before Mock 3.

**Worked example (G6, past or perfect).** Log lines: Day 78 S3 *Jag har beställt den i måndags*; Day 79 W3 *Jag har hyrt bilen den 10 mars*; Day 80 call *Jag har varit där förra veckan*. Seen 3 times: red. The rule: a finished time (*i måndags, den 10 mars, förra veckan, för två år sedan*) takes the past; no time, or "up to now", takes the perfect. Drill, 10 sentences, 5 of each:
1. *Jag beställde den i måndags.*
2. *Jag hyrde bilen den 10 mars.*
3. *Reparatören kom i fredags.*
4. *Jag har redan betalat.*
5. *Den har inte fungerat sedan i lördags.*
Then 5 more of your own, with this week's topics.

**Why it matters for the exam:** Complaints and reports always tell what happened and when, so a past-or-perfect error appears in almost every formal text.

**How we learn it (60 min):**
- 0–6 Review: cards from Days 51, 67, 74, 78 and 80.
- 6–10 Read the set C sheets (or count yourself: provisional); pick the top 3.
- 10–25 The 3 drills.
- 25–39 Redo the 2 weakest set C speaking tasks with new details.
- 39–52 Redo the weakest set C writing task (13 min).
- 52–57 Notice routine, shortened.
- 57–60 Error log.

**Speaking output:** for example S3 with a wrong-size jacket instead of the wrong item, and S4 with a bus instead of a train.

**Writing output:** the weakest set C text with one new detail.

**Listening and reading:** notice routine.

**Done when:** 3 drills with every sentence correct; all 3 redos cover every point; no drilled error appears in the redos.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- *Jag flyttade hit för två år.* → *Jag flyttade hit för två år sedan.* (G9)
- *Jag bor här sedan 2024.* → *Jag har bott här sedan 2024.* (G6)
- Drilling only the error you like fixing. The count decides.

### Day 82: Mock 3 preparation, light (Phase 4, week 12)

**What we learn today:** no new content. The pre-mock warm-up (3.3): five random prompts, each from a different engine (seen practice), first two sentences only; then the 10 core reactions and the 12 core reason pairs from the English cue. Then one short task of each speaking type, on old cards with one detail changed, and one clip.

**Helper's preparation for Mock 3 (do it today, and do not show the learner):**
- Prepare fresh prompts for Mock 3 (plan 6.2, fresh-prompt rule): the teacher's; otherwise one held-back book task per mock slot (book map, "Held back for mocks"); only as a last resort, prompts an AI writes on the day. Put them face down. The Mock 3 set printed in plan 5.1 is seen practice and does not count.
- If the target pair includes listening or reading: pick 2 new easy-news clips and 2 new notices and write about 8 questions for each in the three formats (multiple choice, true/false, short open answers). Without these, Mock 3 gives no booking evidence for listening or reading (6.2).
- Ready a timer, a recorder, a text editor with spelling help off, paper for preparation notes, and a quiet room for 2 to 2.5 hours.

**Why it matters for the exam:** A fixed first sentence is the plan's first rescue rule against freezing (2.8); warm templates make every first sentence automatic tomorrow and the day after.

**How we learn it (about 45 min):**
- 0–8 Review: cards from Days 52, 68, 75, 79 and 81.
- 8–18 Five random prompts, each from a different engine: first two sentences only.
- 18–24 The 10 core reactions and the 12 core reason pairs, fast.
- 24–35 One short task of each type: Berätta 45 s; a call of 3 turns; a voicemail; one reaction; an opinion of 45 s.
- 35–45 One clip, easy-news routine.

**Speaking output:** the 5 short tasks above, recorded; tick the points only; no redo.

**Writing output:** none.

**Listening and reading:** one new clip.

**Done when:** the five prompts take 10 minutes or less; at least 9 of the 10 core reactions and 11 of the 12 core reason pairs are right (the Day 55 marks); the helper confirms the Mock 3 kit is ready.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- Learning anything new today. Recall only.
- Doing a full set today. Save the stamina for Day 84.

**Book practice:** none today. Since 16 November 2026 this day's book tasks are held back for mocks (book map, "Held back for mocks").

---

### Day 83: rest (Phase 4, week 12)

**What we learn today:** nothing. 20 minutes of due cards only (the calendar's rest day). No recording, no writing, no mock prompts.

**Why it matters for the exam:** Mock 3 counts towards booking (6.4); it should measure your Swedish, not your tiredness.

**How we learn it (20 min):**
- 0–20 Due cards, said aloud.
- Then check two things: the helper has the Mock 3 kit ready (Day 82), and you know when tomorrow's 2 to 2.5 hours start.

**Speaking output / Writing output / Listening and reading:** none.

**Done when:** the due cards are done and nothing else was studied.

**Typical errors to watch:**
- A "quick" extra mock or a new word list. Both break the rest day.

---

### Day 84: Mock 3 (Phase 4, week 12)

Day 84: see section 5.1 of the plan.
A full mock of 2 to 2.5 hours on fresh prompts (plan 6.2: teacher-written first, then held-back book tasks, then AI-written on the day; the Mock 3 set printed in plan 5.1 is seen practice): speaking S1–S6 (about 30 min, seller call in S2), a break, writing W1 15 / W2 25 / W3 20 min typed, a break, then 2 clips and 2 notices (20 min), and the count with the teacher pack.
The pass rules are 6.4. Mock 3 is the first mock that counts towards the booking gate, which needs two passes in a row at least one week apart (4.7); the next mock is in the first week of "After Day 84".

[Unreviewed Swedish: have a teacher check it]

---

## After Day 84: the repeat cycle and the taper

### When this applies

- **Mock 3 alone cannot meet the booking gate.** The gate needs two full mocks in a row, at least one week apart, on fresh prompts, both passing 6.4 for the subtests you will book (4.7). So at least one more week always follows Day 84.
- **Section 4.6 sets the rule:** if the gate is not met after Day 84, repeat Days 64–84 with new prompts; do one full mock every week instead of every 4 weeks; after each mock, spend 3 fix days on that mock's top errors in the Day 57 format; keep cycling until the gate is met at a registration window.
- Phase 4 has no other exit. Its exit is the booking gate (4.5).

### The cycle week [Plan's own split of the 4.6 rule]

| Cycle day | What you do | Time |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Full mock** (6.2), fresh prompts, recorded and typed. The teacher rates first, then you count (6.3). | 2–2.5 h |
| 2 | **Fix day 1, Day 57 format** (error cards, engine drill, a speaking redo, a writing task, the log). Focus: say this mock's errors aloud, wrong then right; 2 new correct sentences for each top-3 error type; redo the weakest speaking task on a new card; one new text of the weakest written type (15 min). Mark red types (3.6). | 60 min |
| 3 | **Fix day 2, Day 57 format.** Focus: 3 five-minute drills built with the Day 67 recipe; redo the second-weakest speaking task and the weakest writing task with new details. | 60 min |
| 4 | **Fix day 3, Day 57 format.** Focus: the mock's weakest function, as on Day 58: the drill, then 3 speaking prompts and 1 writing prompt you have not practised (question-bank book tasks, or seen practice with the details changed). | 60 min |
| 5 | **Next day of the Phase 4 rotation** (below), with new prompts. | 60–80 min |
| 6 | **The day after it in the rotation.** | 60–80 min |
| 7 | **Light day** (3.1): due cards, one task re-recorded with a new detail, one clip; then the pre-mock warm-up (3.3: five random prompts from different engines, the 10 core reactions, the 12 core reason pairs). The helper prepares next week's mock. | 30–40 min |

- **The rotation** is the Phase 4 content days in order: 66, 69, 68, 74, 75, 80, 64 or 78, 65 or 79, then start again. New prompts for the rotation days come from the book map's question bank, or from seen practice with the details changed. The mock, fix and light days (67, 70–72, 76–77, 81–84) are already inside the cycle.
- If your target pair includes listening or reading, put Day 66 or 75 into every cycle week [Plan's own], because only teacher- or helper-written questions give booking evidence (6.2).
- Cards follow 3.3 as before. The mock day makes no new cards except error cards. The weekly teacher pack is the mock itself.

### Where the prompts come from

- **Every printed prompt is seen practice** (plan 6.2, decided 16 November 2026): the appendix, the plan's day entries and this file. Use them on fix days and rotation days with the details changed, never as a mock that counts.
- **Each weekly mock that counts needs fresh prompts,** in this order:
  1. **The teacher writes them** (or briefs a helper), one task per row of 6.2: S1 a person or an experience; S2 a seller call one week and a buyer call the next, with 6 voice lines; S3 a complaint or repair call with 6 voice lines; S4 a voicemail; S5 three reactions; S6 an opinion; W1 an informal message; W2 a letter with at least three reasons; W3 a bill complaint, a product complaint or a request, in rotation; and the listening and reading items with about 8 questions each in the three formats.
  2. **Otherwise a helper draws from the 40 held-back book tasks** (book map, "Held back for mocks"), one per slot. They hold no voicemail and almost no seller, buyer or repair call, so those slots still need the teacher or step 3. They last about three to four mocks.
  3. **Only as a last resort, an AI writes the prompts on the day,** one per 6.2 slot; the helper reads them out, and the learner does not see them before the task.
- The learner never writes their own mock prompts.
- **Appendix prompts not yet used anywhere in this file** (still seen practice, but the least practised, so use them first on rotation and fix days): the Day 58 menu rows not used on Days 58, 59 or 76, plus A4, F7, F8, G1, G2, G4, G7, G9, I1, I4, I5, I7, I9, I10, J4 and J10. Almost none are seller, buyer or repair calls.
- **Close to tasks already done** (use them last): H10, K4, G5 and B5 (close to Mock 3), and A5, C7, E1, F9, G6, H1, I2, J2, J7, J8, K2, L1 and L6 (close to tasks in parts 1–3).

### After each weekly mock: the booking check (4.7)

1. Count the mock by 6.3; where your count and the teacher's differ, the teacher's stands.
2. Compare with every row of 6.4 for the subtests you will book: every required point; every task in time; engine errors per text counted and tracked (not a pass rule); a teacher rating of A or B on every task; about 70% or more on teacher- or helper-written listening or reading questions, if that subtest is in your pair. Write "pass" or "fail" and the date in the progress record.
3. The gate is met when **all** of these are true: two passes in a row, at least one week apart, on fresh prompts; both mocks within the 4 weeks before you pay [Plan's own]; Migri's written confirmation, if you plan to combine two partial-day certificates; every item of the 8.3 checklist ticked.
4. Choose what to book with the pair table in 4.7.
5. At each registration window listed in 4.7 (confirm every date and fee before paying), ask one question: **is the gate met today?** If not, let the window pass and keep cycling. Registration is binding [Official].

- With no outside rater you may not book (3.5).
- If the same function fails in two mocks running, spend one teacher session on it [Plan's own, extending the 4.3 rule for a function that fails twice].

### Phase 5: the 7-day taper (section 4.6)

Use it only in the last 7 days before a sitting you have **booked**, whatever the date. It replaces the cycle for those days: no mock and no fix days [Plan's own reading of 4.6]. Use old cards with one detail changed, or leftover appendix prompts. Nothing new is learned.

| Days before the test | Exactly what you do |
|---|---|
| 7 | One timed task of each type: Berätta about a person (1 min prep + 1.5 min); a seller call (6 turns × 20 s); a late voicemail (20 s); three reactions (20 s + 25 s each); W1, an informal message (15 min). All due cards. No new grammar, no new templates. About 60 min. |
| 6 | Berätta about an experience; a complaint or repair call; a cancel voicemail; three reactions; W3, a bill complaint with which bill, two reasons, an amount and contact details (20 min). All due cards. About 60 min. |
| 5 | Berätta about a place or your home; a buyer call; a late voicemail with a new reason; three reactions; W2, a letter with at least three reasons (25 min). All due cards. About 65 min. |
| 4 | Berätta about a person (a different one); a request or booking call; a cancel voicemail; three reactions; W3, a formal request with contact details (20 min). All due cards. About 60 min. |
| 3 | Berätta about an experience (a different one); a repair call; a voicemail; three reactions; your weakest written type from the last mock (15–25 min). All due cards. About 60 min. |
| 2 | Say the core frames aloud once (plan section 1). Do one short timed task (for example a 20-second voicemail). Pack valid photo ID: a passport, ID card or residence permit card, not a driving licence [Official]. About 25 min. |
| 1 | 20 minutes of light review only. Plan the journey with a time buffer: latecomers cannot sit the test [Official]. Pack food and water. Sleep. |
| Test day | Leave your phone and watch: devices are collected [Official]. Read every instruction twice. Answer every item. Never leave a task blank. |

- Days 7–3 are the 4.6 row "one timed task of each type per day". Spreading the three written types across those days is this part's choice [Plan's own].
- Keep the daily 10-minute listening or reading routine on Days 7–3; section 3.7 says not to skip it, and it is not new content.
- If you are ill on the test morning: a refund is possible only for your own illness, with a medical certificate, if you cancel by 08:00 on the test morning [Official] (4.7).

*End of part 3. Before you drill any Swedish here, a teacher must check it.*
